{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nSWE-bench Lite end-to-end evaluation framework for StructFuse.\\n\\nEvaluates a trained retriever on SWE-bench Lite (300 instances):\\n  1. Load SWE-bench instances\\n  2. For each instance: retrieve top-k files -> LLM agent generates patch -> verify\\n  3. Compute resolve rate with 95% bootstrap CI\\n\\nInputs:\\n  --retriever-checkpoint: path to trained retriever checkpoint\\n  --strategy: hard negative strategy (random / embedding_distance / dataflow_aware)\\n  --output-dir: where to write results.jsonl and summary.json\\n\\nOutputs:\\n  {output-dir}/results.jsonl  -- per-instance results\\n  {output-dir}/summary.json   -- aggregate metrics + config\\n\\nDependencies: torch, transformers, datasets, numpy, tqdm\\n  generate_patch additionally requires: openai (for API mode)\\n\\nLLM patch generation backends (set via env vars):\\n  STRUCTFUSE_LLM_BACKEND=mock   (default, returns empty patch)\\n  STRUCTFUSE_LLM_BACKEND=api    (OpenAI-compatible API)\\n  STRUCTFUSE_LLM_API_BASE=...   (API endpoint URL)\\n  STRUCTFUSE_LLM_API_KEY=[REDACTED]    (API key)\\n  STRUCTFUSE_LLM_MODEL=...      (model name override)\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport random\\nimport subprocess\\nimport sys\\nimport tempfile\\nimport time\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom tqdm import tqdm\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Bootstrap CI\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef compute_bootstrap_ci(results: list, n_resamples: int = 10000,\\n                         confidence: float = 0.95, seed: int = 42) -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compute resolve rate with 95% bootstrap confidence interval.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)\\n    n = len(results)\\n    arr = np.array(results, dtype=float)\\n    point_estimate = np.mean(arr)\\n\\n    boot_estimates = np.empty(n_resamples)\\n    for i in range(n_resamples):\\n        sample = rng.choice(arr, size=n, replace=True)\\n        boot_estimates[i] = np.mean(sample)\\n\\n    alpha = 1 - confidence\\n    ci_lower = float(np.percentile(boot_estimates, 100 * alpha / 2))\\n    ci_upper = float(np.percentile(boot_estimates, 100 * (1 - alpha / 2)))\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"resolve_rate\\\": float(point_estimate),\\n        \\\"ci_lower\\\": ci_lower,\\n        \\\"ci_upper\\\": ci_upper,\\n        \\\"n_instances\\\": n,\\n        \\\"n_resamples\\\": n_resamples,\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Data loading\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef load_swebench_instances(dataset_name_or_path: str, max_instances: int = 0) -> list:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load SWE-bench Lite instances.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    from datasets import load_dataset\\n\\n    if os.path.exists(dataset_name_or_path):\\n        ds = load_dataset(\\\"json\\\", data_files=dataset_name_or_path, split=\\\"train\\\")\\n    else:\\n        ds = load_dataset(dataset_name_or_path, split=\\\"test\\\")\\n\\n    instances = list(ds)\\n    if max_instances > 0:\\n        instances = instances[:max_instances]\\n\\n    print(f\\\"Loaded {len(instances)} SWE-bench instances\\\")\\n    return instances\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Retriever\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\nclass _Retriever:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Lightweight StructFuse bi-encoder wrapper for inference.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.35}}"}
```

confidence\\n    ci_lower = float(np.percentile(boot_estimates, 100 * alpha / 2))\\n    ci_upper = float(np.percentile(boot_estimates, 100 * (1 - alpha / 2)))\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"resolve_rate\\\": float(point_estimate),\\n        \\\"ci_lower\\\": ci_lower,\\n        \\\"ci_upper\\\": ci_upper,\\n        \\\"n_instances\\\": n,\\n        \\\"n_resamples\\\": n_resamples,\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Data loading\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef load_swebench_instances(dataset_name_or_path: str, max_instances: int = 0) -> list:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load SWE-bench Lite instances.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    from datasets import load_dataset\\n\\n    if os.path.exists(dataset_name_or_path):\\n        ds = load_dataset(\\\"json\\\", data_files=dataset_name_or_path, split=\\\"train\\\")\\n    else:\\n        ds = load_dataset(dataset_name_or_path, split=\\\"test\\\")\\n\\n    instances = list(ds)\\n    if max_instances > 0:\\n        instances = instances[:max_instances]\\n\\n    print(f\\\"Loaded {len(instances)} SWE-bench instances\\\")\\n    return instances\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Retriever\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\nclass _Retriever:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Lightweight StructFuse bi-encoder wrapper for inference.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.35}}"
    }
  }
}

 raw = response.choices[0].message.content or \\\"\\\"\\n        except Exception as e:\\n            print(f\\\"[generate_patch] API call failed: {e}\\\")\\n            return \\\"\\\"\\n    else:\\n except SyntaxError:\\n                        subprocess.run(\\n                            [\\\"git\\\", \\\"checkout\\\", \\\"--\\\", \\\".\\\"],\\n                            cwd=repo_path, The issue is that HuggingFace is unreachable even with `network_turbo`. It loaded the dataset from cache initially, but then later it's trying to access HuggingFace again - probably for a different purpose (maybe loading the dataset script again or something). The `HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1` should prevent this, but it seems like the datasets library is still trying to reach the hub.

Wait, looking more carefully at the log - this looks like the same log from the first run. The PID 67402 process is running (59 seconds), but the log might still be from the old process. 

Actually, the second nohup redirects to the same file `/tmp/e2e_dryrun.log`, overwriting it. The log shows "Using the latest cached version" from offline mode, then "Loaded 5 instances" and the model loading, but then HuggingFace errors appear again - likely from the datasets library trying to execute the dataset loading script even with `HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1` set. Looking at the timestamps, I need to trace through when these errors actually occur.

The real issue is that `source /etc/network_turbo` only configures HTTP proxies, not HTTPS, and `HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1` alone isn't preventing all network calls. I should try setting `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` and `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` together to fully isolate the huggingface_hub library, since the script uses `load_dataset` which depends on that library's network behavior.

Looking at the error patterns, there are two distinct requests happening: the initial metadata check for the README, then a separate attempt to download the dataset processing script itself. Even with offline mode enabled, the datasets library seems to be making a second call to fetch the script, which suggests I need to set all three offline flags to completely prevent any hub access.

But examining the logs more carefully, the dataset actually loaded successfully ("Loaded 5 SWE-bench instances"), the model initialized, and evaluation began, so these connection errors might be happening in a different part of the pipeline that's handling them gracefully. Let me check what the actual results show to see if the evaluation completed despite these warnings.

```text
OK, `network_turbo` sets HTTP/HTTPS proxy. The problem is that the proxy was set, but the HuggingFace connection is still failing with "Network is unreachable". This could be:
1. The proxy itself is down
2. The `export` inside nohup isn't working correctly (shell issue)
3. HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1 is set but the datasets library still tries to ping the hub

Let me try a different approach:
1. Set `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` (newer env var that the hub library respects)


2. Also set `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1`
3. Make sure the proxy env vars are actually being exported in the subshell

The real issue is that chaining multiple `&&` operators with `source` and `export` inside `nohup` is fragile. I should write a wrapper script instead to handle all the environment setup cleanly.
```
